ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u4	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the applicants... [[Where are your friends?]] ...I have the honour to present a parliamentary bill amending the Law on the Public Prosecutor's Office and some other laws contained in print no. 2731. [[And where are these applicants?]] The Law on the Public Prosecutor's Office entered into force on 4 March 2016 and has therefore been in force for over 2 years. The main purpose of this law was to restore the solution according to which the Minister of Justice held the office of Attorney General. We foretold this at a time when in 2010 the then ruling coalition abolished this solution. At the time, we signaled the dangers that would be linked to the separation of the office of the Attorney General and the Minister of Justice, and at the same time we announced that when we won the election, this solution would be restored. It is a pity that today, when you are in opposition, you do not accept similar tactics. If we disagree with the solution proposed by the ruling majority... [[Because he disagrees with the constitution.]] ...it is worth offering the public an alternative to what the ruling majority is proposing and waiting for elections, whether the society will support the opposition's proposals or not. Our proposal was supported by society, as a result of which is this new law on the prosecutor's office. [[Voice from the hall: What society?]] But like I said... [[Voice from the room: You didn't consult!]] Like I said, about the Attorney General... [[You didn't say anything about it in the campaign.]] ...was our announcement of joining these positions: the Minister of Justice and the Attorney General, and we have done so. However, of course, two years of the Law on the Public Prosecutor's Office show that some adjustments are needed, which will aim primarily at improving the prosecution's operation and thus even more intense the fight against crime and increasing the security of citizens. For the main purposes of the draft law on the prosecutor's office, these proposals, which are included in it, include: to determine the nature and extent of the prosecutor's participation in proceedings conducted by public authorities and administrations, the courts and tribunals in which his participation is based on the provision of the Article. 5 Laws on the D.A.'s Office. It is also about clarifying the rules relating to the performance by the regional prosecutors of financial and administrative services of the local departments of the Department for Organised Crime and Corruption of the National Public Prosecutor's Office and extending the procedural legitimacy of prosecutors acting in the military departments of the District Prosecutors' Offices and in the military departments of the District Prosecutors' Offices to appear before military garrison courts and military district courts. It is also important to mention the introduction of a solution relating to the organisation and scope of the functioning of the secretariats and other departments of administration in the Main Commission for the Investigation of Crimes against the Polish Nationals and in the Branch Commissions for the prosecution of crimes against the Polish people, as well as the development of terminology and language changes in the content of the article. 93 § 4 Laws on the Public Prosecutor's Office and on the creation of regulations concerning the extension of the possibility of posting prosecutors to the Institute of National Remembrance. It also refers to the definition of a provision providing for the possibility of applying the rules referred to in Article. 109 § Points 1 and 2 and § 2 Rights of the Prosecutor's Office to the Public Prosecutor of the National Public Prosecutor's Office residing outside the locality established by that office unit of the Public Prosecutor's Office and indicating directly in the statutory authorisation provided for in Article. 112 Prosecution rights may be defined in a regulation issued on this basis in cases where the diet referred to in Article is not available. 109 § 1 Laws on the D.A.'s Office. It is also a matter of introducing the possibility for the Prosecutor-General to appeal against the order to discontinue disciplinary proceedings where he is not the initiating party to the investigation and to amend the Article. 174 § 1a point 1 of the Law on the Prosecutor's Office by indicating at the same time that the requirements concerning the applicant for the appointment as an assistant of the Prosecutor's Office are also fulfilled by the person who was employed for at least 3 years as assistant prosecutor or assistant judge. These proposals meet the expectations of the public prosecutors, but also result from an analysis of the functioning of the present law. With regard to the amendments to the Law on the Law on the System of General Courts contained in the amendment of that Law, a number of changes are proposed with varying degrees of relevance and detail aimed at improving the functioning of the universal judiciary. In this case, too, this proposal is the result of an analysis of the application of the current solutions, including recently introduced ones, and serves to correct them in such a way as to eliminate obstacles to the smooth conduct of judicial proceedings and to ensure an even more effective administrative environment for the functioning of the general judiciary. [[What a lie.]] Among the most important changes that are moving in this direction, e.g. amendment of the content of the Article. 11 § 3 of the Law on the System of General Courts, which provides for... [[You'll be legally responsible for this before the court...]] ...allowing the chairman of the regional court to appoint the heads of departments in the court he directs - which is currently the responsibility of the President, the district court's superior - in order to implement the supervision of the subjugated court... [[These courts will take care of you.]] ...by the President of the regional court having responsibility for the proper functioning of that court in the field of judicial administration. Amendments to Article 12 and 16 lead to a more flexible administration of the courts by derogating from the principle that each district and district court must divide into civil and criminal departments. This will allow for the creation of uniform courts specialising in a particular branch of law for the same areas. In order to ensure greater efficiency in the administration of the court, Article is also being amended. 22a (1) which entitles the President of the regional court to determine the division of proceedings in that court. In addition, in Article 2, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: 28 changes have been proposed to allow colleges of courts to vote not only during meetings but also on a regular basis in cases where there is no objection of any of its members against such a form of voting, and therefore when the matter being dealt with by the College is so simple and unambiguous that it is not required to meet physically and to adopt a resolution. Amendments to Article 33 § 5 and Art. 35 § 7 aim to eliminate the quorum at meetings of general judges of appeals and judges of the district as a condition for the effective adoption of resolutions in order to ensure the effective operation of those judicial authorities. In addition, the rules for participation in the competition laid down in Article have been amended. 57 and 57aa, which provide both for the possibility of simultaneous participation in two procedures for appointment to serve as a judge in a general court - so far this has only been possible in the scope of one competition - and allow for effective resignation from the application for a vacant position already as a result of a declaration of will on this subject. With regard to changes in the national judicial school, this is a long-awaited proposal to introduce a complementary application. This solution is expected by referenda of the court, assistants of judges and prosecutors. The organisation of this application shall be assumed on a similar basis as in the case of the current judicial and prosecutorial applications. This is provided for persons who have at least 2 years of experience in the profession of referendator or assistant. The selection for the judge's application will take place by means of a competition, and for the prosecutor's application by a national prosecutor. The application will last for 18 months, which is half as short as the previous one, and the activities will be carried out on weekends so as not to prevent applicants from providing work. The changes which at the moment raise the greatest discussion are changes to the Supreme Court Act and changes to the National Judicial Council Act. The main objective of the amendment to the National Judicial Council and the Supreme Court Act is to improve the proceedings conducted by the National Courts of Justice in matters of appointment to serve as a judge, including the Supreme Court Judge. [[You want your friends.]] Amendment of Article The 31th Act on the National Court Register consists of allowing the Chairman of the Board to appoint teams for the preparation of individual cases to be considered at a meeting of the Council with a wider membership than three persons. Amendments to Article 34 in turn, ensure that the possibility of obstruction of nomination proceedings is excluded by the introduction of a standard indicating that an appeal against a resolution on the application for appointment of a judge does not exclude its legitimacy in so far as it decides to submit an application for appointment to serve as a judge of the Supreme Court, but only if it is taken into account gives the candidate the opportunity to take his application into account in the nearest unfinished nomination proceedings. Those amendments also allow the national judicial board to reject those appeals which have been submitted after the deadline and do not comply with formal requirements or for other reasons remain inadmissible. As regards amendments to the Law on the Supreme Court, they guarantee the candidate to serve as a judge of the Supreme Court the possibility of submitting additional documents in the nomination proceedings to indicate the qualifications he holds, while at the same time introducing a rigour of leaving without identifying those applications which do not meet the requirements or have been made by persons who do not fulfil the explicit formal criteria to take up the position or have been submitted after a period of time, with a view to unambiguous regulation of the procedure in such cases. On the other hand, the new wording of the article. 111 § 5 serves to enable a faster, more beneficial, for the operation of the Supreme Court, to lead to the appointment of the first Supreme Court President after the seat of 2/3 of the Supreme Court judges. [[Shame on you.]] Notwithstanding the above, the Law on the Supreme Court introduced a previously described amendment providing for the maintenance of the current legal status as regards the admissibility of only one position in that court at the same time. No amendment of Article. 31 § 2 of that Law, in relation to the reference to the Unregulated Law on the Supreme Court to the Law on the System of General Courts, could give rise to the claim that the Supreme Court may also apply for more than one vacant position at the same time. In turn, the amendments relating to Article. 117 The Law on the Supreme Court serves to mitigate the effects of the entry into force of the laws ordering judges who also have the nationality of a foreign state to renounce citizenship under the conditions of termination of the service relationship, because they give the judge the opportunity to maintain the occupied judicial position without surrendering Polish citizenship. In turn, the transitional provisions of the Act provide for the possibility of applying new rules which improve the nomination procedure introduced by the Act also to proceedings for appointment to serve as a judge, initiated and not completed before its entry into force. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u36	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! In the draft law amending the Law on the Public Prosecutor's Office and some other laws, you propose amendments to the Act of 8 December 2017 on the Supreme Court in a matter which I am very close to, i.e. citizenship of judges. This case was the subject of several of my speeches last year and repeated speeches to various bodies and institutions. I regret that, ladies and gentlemen, but starting with President Duda and ending with the ministry, Minister, until today, and this is a few or even several months, I have received no reply. I therefore carefully read the proposal to amend the article. 117, which provides for the addition of § 3, 4 and 5. What do they predict? The judge or judicial assessor who, on the date of entry into force of this Act, does not fulfil the requirement to have only Polish citizenship, may, before the expiry of the time limit referred to in § Article 1 117, i.e. by 4 October this year, submit an application to the National Council of the Court of Justice for consent to the appointment of a judge despite having the nationality of another State. The National Judicial Council may consent to a judge's position in spite of the nationality of a foreign State, having regard in particular to the circumstances relating to the acquisition of citizenship and the consequences resulting from his resignation. The National Judicial Board shall issue a resolution within 3 months of the date of impact of the request and shall not have any appeal from that decision to the judge or assesor. We read further: in the case of a resolution issued by the National Court of Justice refusing to allow the appointment of a judge despite the possession of a foreign nationality, the judge or the judicial assessor shall submit to the National Council of the Court, within 2 months of the date of the resolution, documents confirming the taking of actions to waive the nationality of the foreign State. In the event of an unsuccessful expiry of that period, the service relationship of the judge or the judicial assessor shall expire. The whole design of these regulations raises a lot of questions. What does it mean that the National Court of Justice will have regard to the circumstances relating to the acquisition of citizenship or the consequences resulting from its resignation? ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u172	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The Clean Air programme is of great interest. There are various questions, among others. the most frequently asked question is whether it will apply to the inhabitants of all cities. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u173	Thank you very much. The Minister of Environment, Henryk Kowalczyk, is responsible. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u174	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The problem of clean air or the problem of fighting smog is, of course, known for a long time, and we also see this problem. It was very clearly recognised by the public when the European Court of Justice judgment was published this year on air cleanliness standards violations for the years 2007 to 2015. In fulfilling this judgment, we have presented to the European Commission a programme that has received great interest and satisfaction. What is this program? Firstly, Clean Air is a comprehensive programme, i.e. the main source of pollution is low emissions, i.e. emissions from single-family homes... Of course, there is still emissions in the city centers, especially from public transport, and this smog programme provides for the introduction of electromobility, but it is as if another sector within this programme. However, making efforts to achieve this clean air and reducing emissions from single-family homes is that we are going to propose that owners of single-family homes exchange ovens, heating sources, clean heating - clean heat pumps, electric heating, gas heating, connection to district heating, and where there is no other possibility, also on fifth-generation coal furnaces, i.e. those which do not emit contaminated exhaust gases - i.e. such sources. This element is also accompanied by thermomodernization, i.e. the reduction of energy consumption, which is to warm the exterior partitions, in general, but these are walls, ceilings, as well as the replacement of windows and external doors. An important objective of this scheme is that the heating costs for individual owners of single-family homes after completion of the project should not be greater than before it is implemented. The failure of the existing programmes related to the exchange of heating sources involved inter alia. on the fact that coal heating was changed e.g. for gas or electric, without making thermomodernization, and thus not reducing the demand for thermal energy, hence heating costs increased and very often it was so that house owners returned after a year or two to coal heating. So this program is a comprehensive program. As for the time it takes to run, we're predicting... The programme is practically prepared, it will provide grants for eligible costs, i.e. covering part of the eligible costs for the poorer. According to the World Bank's recommendations, grant levels will range from 90% of eligible costs to 40%, depending on revenue. The lowest is the income of PLN 600 per person in the household, 40% concerns income of PLN 1600 per person in the household, and for income of PLN 1600 per person in the household tax relief is foreseen. We want to reduce the eligible costs from the top, i.e. this upper amount of eligible costs is 53 000. zł. On the average single-family house this is quite enough amount to make thermomodernization, window replacement, as well as purchase a new source of heating, i.e. replace. coal furnace for gas furnace. These eligible costs also include making a gas connection or connection to a district heating network if this district heating network is nearby and this connection is cost-effective. The scale of this program is enormous. We predict that this will be a scale of more than PLN 100 billion, where more than 60 billion are grants, and about 40 billion are loans, because in these eligible costs, in addition to grants at the level mentioned by me just now, will also be for other eligible costs granted to loans. So if someone doesn't have so-called. own contribution, it will be able to obtain own loan for this share. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u250	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you very much for this question, because the issues related to accessibility in rural areas, the construction of local roads, the expansion of connections that will make it possible to reach larger urban centres are government priorities. Let me just remind you that the Responsible Development Strategy places a very strong emphasis on the sustainability of the whole country, in particular on the elimination of development barriers in marginalised areas, which are characterised by worse infrastructure, low transport accessibility. As funds for municipal roads and district roads are very difficult to access under the 2014-2020 perspective, the possibility of funding is very limited. The government programme for the development and competitiveness of the regions has been prepared by supporting local road infrastructure. This programme was adopted in March of this year. It focuses on three types of investment. Firstly, investment in local roads to improve the availability of economic or socio-cultural centres and investment areas for entrepreneurs and the local community. The second area, the second type of projects to be implemented under this programme are investments in local roads or nodes linking these roads to higher lines in order to improve access to socio-economic centres of regional or national importance. The third type of projects for which funding is possible are investments in safety, intersections, bridge facilities, bridges, overpasses, ridges, footbridges, traffic lights, pedestrians and cycle tracks. All these investments are to improve safety. As regards the share of State resources in the overall eligible costs, it varies depending on tax revenue per capita in individual municipalities or powiats and amounts to between 60 and 80% of the costs of carrying out the task. Operational issues under these programmes, i.e. recruitment and verification of applications, are the responsibility of the voivodships. Up to 15 April, applications for funding under special grants from the State budget continued to be selected. After completion we have 421 units of local government, municipalities and powiats, which will be covered by a total of 433 investment tasks. Of these, JST 330 are municipalities, 20 are towns on the county's rights, 71 are powiats, and so above all we tried to support rural areas, small towns, according to what the government declared in its programme. The average value of grants per task is 1150 thousand. zł. The amounts of grants are very different from 64 000. PLN up to 5 million. What effects are expected under this programme? Firstly, the creation of new 46 km of municipal roads, almost 304 km of municipal roads will be rebuilt or renovated, more district roads, because 210 km of district roads, under the program 26 bridges are also planned to be renovated, 230 km of walkways, walking-bike paths and 300 passes for pedestrians with a higher standard of safety. At the moment, we still have 196 projects on the reserve list, and they will eventually be financed if there are savings. In addition to this programme, the Ministry of Infrastructure is currently working on a road fund for local authorities, so it should be assumed that such investments in the future will be financed under this fund. As I have said, at the moment the financing of small road infrastructure is very difficult under European measures, but it should be pointed out that such projects can still be implemented both under the cohesion policy and under the rural development fund. If we are talking about the future, the issue of small road infrastructure will be feasible within the territorial objective, as announced by the committee. We plan that, especially for investments in marginalised areas, this type of projects will focus on measures under the trans-regional programme, i.e. the continuation of actions concerning Eastern Poland, [[♪ The bell ♪]] but there are also discussions on the extension of this programme to less developed areas outside the five Eastern Poland voivodships. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u258	Mrs. Marshal! Given the differences in the development interests of the Member States that we are aware of, which are not a new thing which persist and may be expressed more and more, taking into account also the internal situation prevailing in many Member States, in particular in those countries which are net contributors to the budget of the European Union, these budgetary negotiations will certainly be the most difficult budgetary negotiations in the history of the European Union. This is why the government started preparing for this negotiating battle more than two years ago. More than 2 years ago, an inter-ministerial team was set up, with the involvement of all leading resorts, whose role was to prepare a detailed map of interests, of Polish regional interests, in the budget debate. The role of this team is also today to draw up negotiating scenarios, potential negotiating scenarios, and to analyse the positions of individual Member States and institutions in detail. Let us remember that from now on - from May for sure - the issue of the future of the European Union's multiannual budget is not in the hands of the European Commission, it is in the hands of the Member States, in particular the European Council, which acts unanimously. On this occasion, I would like to express my gratitude, in particular to those ministries that bear the burden, the main burden, the responsibility for sectoral policies. I mean the Ministry of Investment and Development, the Ministry of Agriculture, but also the Ministry of Science and Culture, the Ministry of National Defence, because Poland obviously has its interests in every, even the most retail aspect of the multiannual financial framework. Poland, thanks to these preparations, which, as I say, lasts over two years - and I would like this to sound strong in this House - is well prepared for this debate. The European Council held in February was an expression of this. Poland has presented not only a coherent position but also a comprehensive position in all the relevant areas of the multiannual financial framework. Our rations are shared by many countries. There is not one Polish postulate that would be supported only by Poland. Each of these demands, which they will speak about in a moment, is always supported by a group of countries whose development interests coincide, which has been known for years. Poland is not only showing political leadership in this debate but also conceptual leadership. The proof of this is that mainly thanks to the Polish forces, six conceptual documents were created, non-papers referring to the financial framework itself, but also in detail to cohesion policy, in detail to agricultural policy. These are documents which present not only Poland's position on a given matter - I repeat: there were six of them - but also the position of the countries of the region, not only the Visegrad Group, but also groups of Baltic States, Romania and Bulgaria. Depending on the subject, these coalitions can also be wider. Moreover, this coalition must never come down only to the countries of Central Europe. The same problems, in particular as regards the future of cohesion policy, are noted in many other countries, including the southern part of the European Union. The European Commission - and here I would move on to the evaluation of the documents that were mentioned, presented - contrary to appearances, despite the sometimes repeated statistical errors, does not propose a larger budget of the European Union. This is a breach in history. If we were to use really adequate compilations, comparing the EU-27 budget at fixed prices, it would turn out that the WRE after 2020 would correspond to 1.114% GNI, the European Union's gross national income. The current financial perspective is 1,130%. If we added a development fund that is artificially budgeted there, the current financial perspective would reach 1.169%. EU-27 GNI. In this case, the European Commission proposes a smaller budget. This is very important because we stand clearly and we have said this from the beginning: new ambitious goals cannot be achieved, often very ambitiously outlined, without new money. It is frivolous to suggest that the European Union radically strengthen its commitment to migration or defence policy, which, in principle, we do not have fundamental reservations here, without presenting the way in which these actions are financed. Poland, like many other countries, will not agree with the situation in which this bright plan for the Union to be more active in migration and defence, is financed by simple transfers of national envelopes, development goals which remain valid, which have not become obsolete, which are particularly important for the countries of Central Europe and the countries of the South. This is the reason why Poland has actually proposed that if we are really ambitious towards new goals, we need to think about the income side, about raising the contribution. Of course, this willingness is conditional on agreeing on the political goals for which this money is to go. This is not an unconditional position. We are open to talking about our own resources, provided, of course, that they are spread evenly between Member States. This excludes, at the outset, a discussion on own resources based on, for example, climate revenues. Such a solution, proposed by the committee, would mean that the poorest pay the most, which seems to be a politically unattractive proposal. We also offer to eliminate discounts. The UK's exit from the European Union is an excellent time to end with discounts in the system, the architecture of the multiannual financial framework. The European Commission has not taken this opportunity to address this issue at least. We have a clear position in the committee's position regarding maintaining discounts in the new version. Of course, we understand perfectly the financial consequences of brexite, which must be painful, but we will not agree that these financial consequences of brexite or other constraints in the Member States will result in unsustainable, disproportionate cuts, especially in those policies that are important for countries, regions, including Poland. I mean cohesion policy as well as agricultural policy. Cohesion policy is not a one-sided, one-way, non-refundable loan. This is an investment in the development of the European Union. Thanks to this policy, we are pursuing objectives that are European targets in individual Member States, not any goals, but those agreed by everyone at the EU table. The questioning of this fact is simply populism, and it does not matter whether it is anti-European populism, which some are so eager to talk about, or pro-European populism under the blue flag. Support for agriculture is important not only because it determines the maintenance of our desirable model, which we do not want to abandon, the social, economic model of agriculture, which is important for Europe. Support for agriculture is necessary if we are to maintain public support and understanding for an open trade policy that produces competitive pressures, particularly in the agricultural area, which is probably the most sensitive sector in terms of global pressures. An ambitious global trade policy cannot be imagined without maintaining serious proportional support for agricultural policy. Poland has presented a coherent and, I believe, balanced position on this issue. Indeed, it seems that penalties for breaching safety and food quality requirements should be much more proportionate to the act being committed, as they are currently too low in terms of their effectiveness, which means that they do not fulfil the deterrent function. The draft law also proposes to clarify a number of other provisions aimed at avoiding doubts about controlled and controlled cooperation, such as the possibility of making copies, copies and printouts of documents related to a specific control. At present, this possibility arises only in the case of documents drawn up in a foreign language. We began to notice - and this year's strawberry action culminated - that imported strawberries from European countries, non-European ones, were repacked in Polish packaging to sell them under the banner, under the brand name of Polish strawberries, which was clearly cheating consumers. And that's what this project is about. This project concerns the control of fresh products, unprocessed at the retail stage. However, there is a second phenomenon, which is no less interesting phenomenon, and probably its scale is much larger and even bigger - it is a phenomenon of importing raw materials to Polish food production, which are later mixed with Polish products of excellent quality, and those are of questionable quality and reach consumers under the banner, under the brand name of Polish production, also reach consumers in other European countries, other countries of the world as an excellent quality or good quality Polish product. At this point it is worth paying attention to the calendar of certain events, so that we are aware of the risks that caused this. I remind you, last summer, 2017 - as a result of Ukraine's negotiations with the European Commission, trade in food products is liberalised, a quota is abolished for many products, many products are imported, it is agreed to be imported at zero duty rates. We then pay attention to us as a club of the Polish People's Party at conferences, but also in the European Parliament in the agriculture committee with the mouth of the head of this commission of Czesław Siekierski, that it will be extremely unfavourable for Poland, that it will enable Poland to import, but also to Poland, many food products from Ukraine, cheaper produced, not necessarily of adequate quality. Unfortunately, this happens without opposition from the Polish authorities. Those rules are coming into force. The European Commission accepts them. It did not take long to wait, because only until the summer of this year, until June - July this year, to see the effects of this opening. Then everyone but us underestimated this, and the effects of this today are clearly seen in the example of raspberries and the last actions we are talking about, the actions that are being taken, and what the new Minister of Agriculture has said recently, that we need to increase control of products imported from Ukraine. It turns out that year to year, i.e. from 2017 to 2018, in this time interval I am talking about, imports into Poland of raspberries originating in Ukraine increased by 26% - not these fresh raspberries, but raspberries or frozen raspberries, either in the form of concentrate or pulp, imported into Poland in tanks, imported without any control. And everyone's gonna admit it. Also, the services say that it was not controlled by what preparations these raspberries were sprayed with, what preparations they were fertilized with. They were imported to Poland, to factories, often today Ukrainian, bought or built recently, and later as Polish raspberries or after mixing with Polish raspberries they left, taking the place, as far as the market is concerned, to Polish raspberries. What's the scale? 'Cause somebody might say 26%. It's a small scale. In the past few years, exports of raspberries from Ukraine to Poland have virtually increased from zero to 14 and 15 thousand. t. Overall, exports of raspberries from Ukraine increased from zero to 14,15 thousand. t of which 50% export to Poland. So we see what an excellent channel we are thanks to the regulations that were adopted last summer in order to transport this product to other countries, mixing it with Polish, and this product has gained better quality qualities. Only events that took place recently, in spring - the protests of vegetable producers, then fruit producers - resulted in this project being introduced. We, going in the direction that we need to support provisions that will introduce some positive regulations - but showing this calendar, because I talked about how negative effects can have certain decisions and not react from Poland - we will support this project, hoping that although part of the market it will heal, improve and make Poles more aware and confident that when buying a Polish product, [[♪ The bell ♪]] they really buy a Polish product. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u362	Don't disturb me, ma'am. Sit down. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! During the meeting of the Committee on Public Finance yesterday, there was a reading of the draft law on the Polish Economic Institute, as well as a discussion on it. First of all, I would like to point out that the committee was in favour of adopting this law and referring it to second reading without amendment. 12 Members voted in favour, against - 8, without a abstention. The Act on the Polish Economic Institute aims to establish a research unit, which is a state institute, to replace the existing research institute - the Institute for Market, Consumption and Conjugation Research. It turned out that the existing institute and research of other institutes did not meet such needs as government policy, state policy in the creation, assessment of the economic situation, development strategies, market analysis, and also the international situation. Therefore, the Act introduces a new institute to replace the existing one, and I would like to point out that all tangible and intangible values derived from the existing market research institute, as well as its receivables and liabilities, become assets of the present new institute and its receivables and liabilities. As far as targets are concerned, they are well-edited, concisely formulated. The Institute will also carry out tasks, through the establishment of local branches, which will consist in inspiring, organising, carrying out research in the field of economic and social sciences covering all economic and social processes for the purposes of public authorities, in order to develop both analyses of the current situation and the objectives for the future, for the purpose of macroeconomic stabilisation, etc. He will also prepare analyses, expertise and prognostic studies in this area. He will cooperate with foreign and Polish institutions, science centres and individuals in this field, as well as academic and scientific units both in Poland and abroad. He will also organize trainings and conferences, which is very much needed in view of the constantly changing situation in Poland, as well as in the world of social, demographic and economic situation. The world keeps changing. As the philosophers have said, everything is flowing. Therefore, we need a very efficient and up-to-date analysis of these processes, we need power, the President of the Council of Ministers and the entire power in Poland data and instruments needed to make decisions. The Act shall detail the bodies of the Institute, their appointment and dismissal. It also talks about the financial economy of the institute, which will be maintained by both subjective grants and targeted grants, as well as by means of European Union funds, including commercial activities in the case of various publications, expertise, analyses, and for example. from records, inheritances, donations and other revenues. In accordance with the requirements of the Public Finance Act, the Act is outlined, a certain amount of funds which will be earmarked for the functioning of this institute in the years 2018 to 2027. This is the amount of PLN 15 million. I would like to point out that this is the maximum amount that does not have to be used. Each year, the Sejm will decide what limit of resources will be allocated to the operation of the Institute. During the committee's work a lot of controversy was raised by one of the articles, namely the article. 16, which says that the Institute will have the right to request from the various institutions, which I will immediately exchange, the statistical data necessary to carry out its tasks, of course excluding personal data. In particular, the data collected from the president of the CSO, the Social Insurance Institution, the National Health Fund, the President of the National Bank of Poland, the Minister responsible for public finances and organisational units under his authority and other public authorities supervised by him. He will have the right to request the availability of statistical data. The committee was attended by representatives of the National Bank of Poland, who pointed out that this was a rather sensitive subject, because there could be a conflict of international rules, EU rules, in terms of the provision of such data, with the provisions of the Act. It has been stressed that, yes, certain data may be made available by the institutions mentioned by me, such as the European Central Bank or other international organisations. On the other hand, you may have doubts whether all these data can be made available to the national institution, which is the Polish Economic Institute. From the statements of the representative of the government and the current director of the institute - the statements of the director of the institute, I would stress, very interesting, very factual, very substantive, that the dispelling of various objections and, at the same time, hoping that the current director of the institute has the idea of how to improve it, how to act to best fulfil his tasks - it follows that this problem, which is the issue of possible conflict of laws, will be removed during further work on the project. The Central Statistical Office also made some comments. I also hope that, in the course of further work on this project - as the Committee on Public Finance advocates - these possible threats or uncertainties, dilemmas will be resolved and we will avoid any problems. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u403	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present what measures we have taken to mitigate the effects of this year's drought, which is unfortunately more and more frequent for agriculture in the coming years. Maybe it's actually signum temporalis, a sign of the upcoming climate change. In Poland in May and June there was a accumulation of several phenomena: high sunshine, high temperatures and at the same time small, in some areas of the country practically absent, precipitation. This drought has actually scored all the mistakes of many years in water management, [[Applause]] because the absence of precipitation can be compensated for by adequate soil fertility, water content, also by evaporation from water reservoirs - all that has not been raised in respect of retention for many years. It is enough to say that during the period of the People's Poland meliorations were called merely draining land, and melioration from the Latin word "meliorare" is an improvement. Where you need to dry, you need to use whether you have discovered or closed drainage systems, and where you need to irrigate, you need to have water shots and irrigation systems. Tough, big job ahead of us. It takes many years and a lot of money to rebuild good water management. A retention program will be prepared in the near future. Here I am very hoping to cooperate with the Minister of the Environment and with the Polish Water farm, because only then will we react in such a way that there is no drought, not to fight this drought. However, this phenomenon occurred this year, covering almost the whole of Poland. This phenomenon occurred to a small extent in the mountain belt, where the climate is different and drought is a rare phenomenon. Unfortunately, too, these phenomena have occurred in areas where natural disasters occur in subsequent years. Drought occurred and last year, and 2 years ago in many parts of Poland, but there was also a phenomenon of massive rainfall in the autumn of last year, which resulted in partial melting or flooding. Farmers were not able to go into the field at the right time, therefore the dates of spring sowing were moving, and only when they could enter the fields were sown very late mainly Jare cereals, which turned out to be the most vulnerable and sensitive to drought. In order to provide any assistance to farmers affected by drought, it is first necessary to know the scale of this phenomenon and to determine what damage this drought actually causes. The auxiliary indicator, which was introduced a few years ago - may have to be more objective, but on the basis of it it has been provided in recent years - is the monitoring of drought conducted by the Institute of Cultivation, Fertilization and Soil Science in Puławy, a good Polish scientific and research institute, with experience, which has developed such a method based on rain tests and the course of weather conducted by about 670 meteorological stations. It defines the so-called. climatic water balance, that is, what they are, in some simplification, the relationship between the amount of precipitation and the needs, evaporation or evapotranspiration, which is also evaporation from the surface not only of the ground, but also of plants. This is an indicator that shows where drought can occur in Poland. But what this drought causes depends on many other factors: what kind of soil it is - whether it is strong soils, concise soils, water-holding soils, whether it is very sandy soils, where it could actually rain every day and probably not be too much; also from what kind of plant it is. - whether it is a shallow-rooted plant, whether it is a plant with a large root system, whether it is a plant that enters the growing season as a winter plant. These are all elements that we take into account to assess the effects of this drought on individual types of plants and to apply the appropriate form of aid. In order to estimate this, for many years, also in 2015, when the drought occurred and Prime Minister Kopacz's government also tried to apply some forms of aid, precisely by using the same methods this aid was provided. Since then, we have improved one of the elements, namely the method of estimating on farms that have animals, because the method of estimating so far, even when it was included, made the livestock holdings with a large number of animals never - even if everything on this farm dried up - able to show a percentage of the losses, because it was calculated the value of the entire production. So we improved these methods a little bit, my predecessor did it, I am grateful to him for it, and according to these methods the estimation took place this year in Poland. In order to be able to estimate this, special committees must be set up, they must be appointed by the voivode in agreement with the municipalities, the voivodeships and mayors at their request. There's been a very strange phenomenon this year. Instead of setting up these committees quickly to assess what is happening in the fields as soon as possible and help farmers, many municipalities were not particularly interested in setting up these committees. I took decisive action, conducted video conferences with voivodships, appealed to both self-government and farmers to influence their self-government so that these committees could be set up, because in the current legal state there is no way to assess losses without this stage and start providing aid. I would like to emphasise, because people often mistake this, that climatic water balance is an indicator, where in Poland drought could possibly cause losses. It is not a sufficient tool to use different forms of assistance. The good word and persuasion began to work, and just as on June 13th was 56 committees, on June 20th, when I took over the office of Minister of Agriculture, there were 206 committees, and then it quickly started to grow: June 27 - 693, July 4 - 1359, July 11 - 1792, and at the moment they operate 1890. I understand that in all municipalities where there is a need for estimation, these committees have been set up. Some of them finish their jobs, most of them will be doing it by the end of the week, maybe there'll be some slipping, but with such a huge action, it's understandable. So far, ladies and gentlemen, these 1890 committees, making estimates, have established that today there are people affected by drought - of course there will still be corrections, I am reporting today, but probably the next days will still increase these indicators - 91 600 farms. The crop area today - 1558 thousand. ha and probably even a few hundred thousand hectares will increase after these estimates, the results of which will reach us. The most annoying drought that is specially treated because it's over 70% losses on the holding relate to around 74 000. ha. Ladies and gentlemen, this shows the scale of the phenomenon, shows that it is a disaster that has affected a large area and many farmers in Poland. Fortunately for us, nature turns out to be gracious and rainy, which at the moment are starting to show the other side, when it comes to water scarcity, stopped the destruction of the plants of the late harvest. These plants, which will be harvested only in the autumn, are potatoes, these are beets, these are vegetables, these are berries, these are soft fruit... [[Voice from the room: The raspberries were already there.]] ...they are also orchards, it is also silage in various types of use and use. There is hope that there will be minimal losses here, that the weather will make up for it, because if the drought were to continue in such a dimension as it did for spring cereals, native cereals and rape, it would be an unimaginable tragedy for Poland. Therefore, the grace of nature will probably save us from a dramatic situation that would be a huge blow and could destroy agricultural production, which may take many, many years to rebuild. What forms of aid have been used for a long time? Different. The instrument is different, farmers want to benefit from different forms of aid. It is not true that all farmers expect only aid per hectare or compensation per hectare. I know many farmers who say that compensation to a hectare is more important to them, just for example. For those who have to repay large loans this year, loans previously taken up, also for the disasters that have affected these farms, and this year they are being repaid, it is a much more important tool to be able to spread into new, convenient instalments, the extension of this loan, so that the holding can survive until the next seasons and maintain production. For many farmers who have animal production, this is particularly the case for farmers who have large herds of cattle, the main problem is how to buy feed and where to buy feed in order to maintain the stock and prevent mass slaughter, slaughter of cattle, which would result in a fall in prices and at the same time significant losses on these farms. I came to the European Commission without delay to obtain assistance from the Commission, financial assistance which is possible, but given the fact that drought has occurred in several countries in Europe, I think it will be rather gestures of sympathy and solidarity that the European Commission will be reluctant to start a crisis fund. However, we have been able to negotiate, this is important for those who need feed, to agree that from protected areas within the framework of the so-called. greening this area, which normally cannot be used for harvesting, could be used for feed. So both land that is set aside and land where it is sown can be used for fodder. We have also been given the opportunity as a result of negotiations to speed up payments of advances and to pay people money on a large scale, which is due to future payments, because it is very important for the liquidity of the farms to make this money quickly. From mid-October to the end of November, the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture will pay advance payments of 70% measures for farmers in the first pillar and 85% The second pillar, from the RDP, concerns agri-environment-climate programmes and the ONW. It's a big help that's gonna get to the farmers soon. The quick giver, the two give, you probably know the saying. Some farmers are interested, those who lease their land from the State Treasury, in particular the possibility of redemption or reduction of the lease charges, the rentals they bear due to the lease of the State. They'll report to the National Agricultural Support Centre. There are procedures in place to help these farmers reduce their burden on the Treasury. It is also possible to reduce or cancel contributions to the Agricultural Social Insurance Fund, but this period is a non-contributory period, few farmers want to benefit from it, perhaps young people who do not yet think about securing their old age. Ladies and gentlemen, it is also possible to obtain a loan extension, some of which underestimate this possibility, and for many farmers who need for example. funds for the purchase of feed, the possibility of obtaining a very low interest rate, the new loan is really important. The Council of Ministers will decide that these loans will be interest-bearing at 0,5%, i.e. an absolutely preferential level which will allow farmers to benefit from this loan. In extremely extreme situations, when the income of agricultural families falls, social assistance can also be applied, as has been the case for example. in the Kujawsko-Pomorskie voivodship last year after the nawałnice, which in August affected our voivodship and the Pomorskie voivodship. Ladies and gentlemen, a matter which should be taken seriously by local authorities is the possibility of the remission of one or two instalments of agricultural tax. By referring to my personal experiences, as well as the calls of farmers, I pointed out that the situation on the fields is bad, and the reason for this is a significant shortage of water. In my interpellation, I asked whether the Ministry was planning to declare the state of the natural disaster due to drought, but the local authorities did not always want to accept farmers' requests to set up a committee to estimate the losses caused by drought. I have widely informed farmers about their rights, I have indicated that the municipalities are obliged to accept such proposals. In my parliamentary offices, assistance was offered in fulfilling such requests and all necessary information was provided there. I proposed further solutions in interpellation 23363. I pointed out that the water balance in June was worse than in the worst years, which means that losses can be really significant. During his press conference, the President of the Council of Ministers stressed that the government intends to help farmers with drought and does not want to leave them to themselves. I therefore proposed the extension of the loans made by farmers. I have submitted an interpellation on this matter personally to the Prime Minister. Minister Krzysztof Ardanowski also pointed out during the conference that the government would assist the victims. I therefore called for government units to assess the damage, which would contribute to efficient coordination already at central level. On June 26th, together with local farmers, I organized a press conference on one of the fields near Proszowice. In this particular field there was no second planting of parsley. The farmer suffered nothing but losses, and that was just a tiny drop in the sea of damage. The regional publicisation of the problems was very important at the moment. I also prepared letters to all the voivods in which I asked them to declare the state of the natural disaster. I called on the voivodships to support farmers at this difficult time. [[Applause]] I took another initiative as part of Interpellation 23548. It called for support for farmers by simplifying the procedure for granting disaster loans, so that this could be done on the basis of the invoices shown for seeding materials. or fertilizer. When a damage assessment committee is refused, such credit may save holdings and maintain the continuity of production. On the other hand, in interpellation No 23626, I propose to help farmers by changing the agricultural insurance system. This was also mentioned by Minister Jan Krzysztof Ardanowski. At present, agricultural insurance is perceived by farmers as complicated and expensive. The need for separate crop insurance against drought, hail or rain makes production impossible. I have called for the implementation of a system based on mandatory, cheap and comprehensive agricultural insurance, as has been the case in the past. In turn, in the interpellation No 23627, I raise the issue of strict criteria determining the formal declaration of agricultural drought. These conditions shall be determined by means of a climatic water balance. I have proposed to mitigate the critical climatic water balance for particular species and crop groups. In addition, concerned about the numerous signals reported by farmers, I submitted an interpellation on crop insurance against drought. Farmers indicated that some insurance companies refuse crop insurance for drought. In my interpellation, I asked if the Ministry was considering a possible intervention in this case. I would like to point out that Minister Ardanowski praised today the agricultural programme of Kukiz, 15 by Mr Sachaika. Then use this program, Minister. [[Voice from the room: Questions, not evaluations.]] [[Voice from the room: Questions.]] Ladies and gentlemen, the questions about the insurance system were very interesting. And Mr Dolata and other Members spoke on this subject. Indeed, the percentage of insurance in Polish agriculture is, to put it simply, embarrassingly low. The climate in which we operate and its increasingly dynamic phenomena, year by year, indicate that we should strive for a universal insurance system, of course strongly supported by the state. No other version in Poland will be able to be applied because the contributions that insurance companies expect are not possible to bear by farmers. Many countries, by building such a massive system using state aid, have solved the problems of various types of phenomena that occur every year, e.g. in the South of the United States, Arizona, Texas and others. [[Indemnity insurance.]] The effects of cyclones, tornadoes, hurricanes that haunt those areas are eliminated by insurance, to which the state pays a great deal. In Poland, five insurance companies showed minimal interest in insurance in agriculture. And if you'll excuse me, because one of you has asked what kind of government has the power to pressure and force companies into insurance, these are companies that are not subject to government pressure in the field of insurance. [[The Prime Minister encouraged us not to insure ourselves.]] These insurance companies, with different legal and ownership compositions, must want insurance. Since none of the five companies you probably know are interested in insurance, at most these companies make some gestures in terms of insurance, I made efforts and in the Ministry of Agriculture 2 days ago a special team was set up, in which representatives of these five insurers interested in insurance, but who are afraid to take them because of the great risk they present in agriculture, are talking - and I will very much support - about the creation of a joint insurance company which, using the infrastructure of these companies, would deal with agricultural insurance and would be supported from the state budget to the extent that these agricultural insurances were possible to bear by farmers. This is, I think, the only sensible direction we can try to take in the near future. Trying to build a separate, new insurance company or imposing an obligation on only one of these five agricultural insurance companies, to put it simply, does not seem possible to me, because there will always be only a small proportion of insured farmers. On the other hand, we should strive to ensure that through the universality of insurance, and even as farmers themselves are beginning to suggest, through the obligation of state-supported insurance, that we farmers are in a situation where we will not - we farmers - tremble every year about whether or not a natural phenomenon will destroy our production. [[They had no choice.]] We are dealing here, on the contrary, with narrowing down, not with extending the content of the rules. High Izbo! The first of the package of amendments deletes Article. 1 containing amendments to the Forest Act and its consequence is to amend the title of the Law in the report of the Joint Committees. It sounds right now: the Act amending the Environmental Law. The second amendment brings new regulations to the current Article. 2 by adding a new chapter 5 entitled: Polskie Domy Drewniane Spółka Akcyjna. In the articles added from Article. 421a to Art. 421k contains basic regulations concerning the company Polskie Domy Drewniane SA. The share capital is at least PLN 50 million. Total participation of the founders, i.e. The National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management and the Bank for Environmental Protection, in the share capital of the company and in the total of votes for all shares of the company is more than 50%. This is intended to prevent the sale or loss of majority by public shareholders at the time of possible presentation of the company in the public offer. In turn, the registered shares belonging to the founders may not be sold or charged, except for the sale or charge to the Treasury. Once the status of a public company is obtained, the personal rights of the founders and the preference of registered shares shall not expire. It is these records that protect the company and its assets against unwanted changes in ownership. The scope of the company's activities is, as I have already said, but I will say it once again strongly, energy-efficient wooden construction including building construction, management of these buildings, renting of residential buildings or premises with the possibility of selling them. In addition, the company may acquire land, renovate or rebuild residential buildings, acquire or develop new technological solutions for wood processing, acquire or process wood raw materials and wood products, or carry out other activities related to energy-efficient timber construction. High Izbo! In cases not governed by the provisions contained in the amended Environmental Law, the provisions of the Commercial Companies Code and of the Law of 16 December 2016 on State Property Management are applicable, in accordance with one of the final articles of the project under consideration. Art. 5 of this law contains 14-day vacathio legis. High Izbo! The whole of the bill amending the Environmental Law was adopted by 24 votes in favour with 14 opponents and 1 abstentions. High Izbo! Wooden buildings have a number of advantages: short construction time, energy efficiency, low construction weight, which allows construction in weak areas, durability and durability of the structure, ease of reconstruction and modernization. In many countries these advantages of wooden construction are appreciated. Wood construction is used on a large scale, among others. Finland, Japan, Norway and the United States. In Scandinavia, the level of application of wooden construction is 70-90%. It's used on a really big scale. High Izbo! Wood is the best carbon storage facility. It is better to support wooden construction than to put carbon dioxide under the ground for large public funds and to hope that it will remain there. These numerous advantages of wooden construction after the adoption of the project discussed here will have the opportunity to be used also in Poland, therefore, High Izbo, on behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Infrastructure, please adopt the draft contained in the report of the Joint Committees in print No. 2751. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u575	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present to the High House the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the draft law amending the Law on Forests and the Environmental Law in print No. 2718. The draft law submitted by the government aims to establish the economic organism of the Polish Wooden Houses Akcyjna Spółka. A few words about the project. Timber construction can be one of the factors contributing to increasing the availability of housing for society. Wooden construction is the use of wood resources produced by Polish Forests. It is also an opportunity to develop modern woodworking technologies and its much greater and rational use in the national economy. Very often in large-scale construction - sports halls, swimming pools - wooden girder roof structures are used, unfortunately imported from abroad. Wood construction is also a saving of energy and what is associated with it - the reduction of environmental pollution by so-called. low emissions. In addition, wooden buildings also have many other advantages, such as short construction time - this has already been mentioned - a small weight, the possibility of building in weak areas, durability and durability of the structure, resistance to weather conditions and lack of seasonality in construction. Therefore, this type of construction is worth supporting and promoting alongside construction using conventional materials such as concrete, brick, steel and glass. Wooden buildings are also more friendly to our health and comfort of the apartment. At the same time, wood is natural and renewable, subject to 100% recycling or biodegradable. Wood carries the lowest carbon footprint compared to other building materials - high-energy fossil fuels are not required for wood production, as are the above mentioned materials. Law and Justice fully agree with this approach to timber construction and its dissemination. According to the draft law in the government submission, print No. 2718, the joint stock company Polskie Domy Drewniane was to create State Forests, the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management and the Bank for Environmental Protection, but during the discussion on the final shape of the law we listened with attention to the reservations of the foresters' environment and at the first reading of the draft law at the meeting of the Environment Committee and the Infrastructure Committee were tabled and adopted appropriate amendments, which narrowed the emerging entity to two founders: the National Environmental Fund and the Bank for Environmental Protection. Law and Justice in the making of laws always tries to listen to the voice of society [[Joy in the Chamber]] and include it in its decisions by making appropriate adjustments. We did so this time, taking into account the critical voices of the foresters, taking into account their reservations, although we did not always fully agree with them. [[Don't shoot.]] Law and Justice also considers that entities forming a public limited liability company, i.e. The national environmental fund and the Bank for Environmental Protection, which are by their nature aimed at environmental protection, will guarantee the sustainable use of forest resources. The statutory provision on the capital structure guarantees that the State Treasury's share in the share capital will be 25%, and together with the other founders the State Treasury is legally guaranteed 50% in the capital and votes of the company. The shares of the founding members shall not be disposed of or charged, except for disposals or charges to the Treasury. This means that the proposed solutions ensure that the State Treasury and the other founders retain full control of the company even after its shares were admitted to trading and possible acquisition by private entities. Such action will protect the emerging economic operator from the temptations of potential speculation and will provide the Treasury with full control over the resulting company. High Izbo! While our country is far from the top of the threat countries, such a large gathering of politicians, scientists and representatives of various stakeholders around the world can obviously attract terrorists interested in creating a spectacular effect. This threat must be strongly countered. Potential cyber attacks should also be prevented. High Izbo! In response to these challenges, the draft law, which is the subject of our deliberations, contains the following solutions. First of all, the appointment of a representative of the government for: Presidency of the Conference of the Parties - Secretary of State in the Ministry of Environment appointed by the President of the Council of Ministers, who will carry out the tasks of Poland resulting from the Presidency of the Conference, and which will be supported by the Ministry of Environment. Secondly, the appointment by the Minister of Foreign Affairs of a special envoy of the Minister of Foreign Affairs for Foreign Affairs. the climate that will carry out the tasks associated with the COP organisation. Thirdly, inclusion in the team COP24 new members: Marshal of the Śląskie Voivodship, who manages rail transport in the voivodship, or appointed deputy marshal or member of the Management Board. Fourthly, the financial envelope needs to be increased to cover the costs resulting from the increased number of delegates. It should be noted, however, that it is Poland which finances to a large extent the costs of participation in the conference of representatives of countries so-called. non-privileged, i.e. those who cannot afford to send their delegation and cover the costs involved. The transport costs of the participants are increasing, among others. because most of the accommodation in the Śląskie voivodship has already been used and delegates will reside in the Małopolska voivodship, which requires efficient transport. In addition, it is necessary to increase the financing of security by covering the payment of the equivalent of money to police officers, State Fire Department, State Security Service, Border Guard and Military Police Soldiers who will serve from 30 November to 15 December of this year in a time exceeding the statutory standard of service time. High Izbo! The first reading of the draft law took place at a meeting of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, followed by a detailed work on the draft. Three amendments were introduced in the draft law during this work. The first amendment extends from 1 September to 1 October. the deadline by which the Silesian voivode will draw up a plan to ensure security and public order during the COP24 conference. The development of this plan requires close cooperation with the United Nations Security Department and institutions involved in security in Poland. The second amendment concerns an increase in the expenditure limit for the co-financing of COP24 conferences and the performance of tasks related to the role of the Polish Presidency of the Conference of the Parties - up to PLN 252 million, of which up to PLN 225 894 948 will be issued in 2018, i.e. an increase of 28 million compared to the bill. However, in 2019 no more than PLN 26 105 052 will be released, i.e. an increase of 3 million compared to the project presented. High Izbo! It is not concluded that this money will be spent in its entirety, but at the stage of public procurement procedures it is necessary to have the appropriations provided in the expenditure limit. The amounts not released will be returned to the authorising officer's account. In particular, great sums are consumed by ensuring the security of the conference participants, with cybersecurity being particularly difficult to assess. The third amendment concerns the extension of the list of entities entitled to the equivalent of money for the service in excess of a statutory standard of service time for soldiers of the Military Gendarmerie. The amendment gives the Minister of Defence the opportunity to conclude an agreement with the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management to transfer funds for payment of this equivalent. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u683	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Health Committee, I have the honour to present to the High House a report on the draft government bill amending the Anti-drugs Act and the State Sanitary Inspection Act, print No. 2746. On 17 July 2018, the Marshal of the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Health Commission for first reading. Today, the Health Commission carried out its first reading, examined the project. The committee's report is print 2780. High Izbo! The proposed amendment to the Law on the Prevention of Drug Addiction has been developed due to the need for further measures to limit the production and placing on the market of substitutes and new psychoactive substances. The amendment also takes into account the new legal solutions adopted in the European Union to include new psychoactive substances in the risk assessment procedure at European Union level and the procedure for subjecting the substance to control measures and criminal penalties as provided for in national drug law. Therefore, the purpose of the proposed law is to include in particular criminal legislation acts relating to the placing on the market of these substances and to create legal instruments enabling the criminal liability of persons associated with it. In view of the rapid rate of occurrence of dangerous substances, it is considered appropriate to list them in a regulation of the Minister of Health where the time necessary for legislative work is shorter than when legislative amendments are made. This will enable society to be better protected from these substances. The draft regulation of the Minister of Health assumes that drugs, in addition to these drugs and psychotropic substances, Annexes 1 and 2, will also be new psychoactive substances, Annex 3. The annex will include a list of substances which the team for assessing the risks to human health or life associated with the use of new psychoactive substances operating at the Minister of Health recommends as substances dangerous to health and life and causing social harm. The proposed amendment introduced by the bill will be an obligation for entrepreneurs to obtain permission to carry out legal activities with the use of new psychoactive substances. In the case of activities relating to the manufacture, processing, processing, import, export, intra-Community supply or intra-Community acquisition and the placing on the market of a new psychoactive substance, operators carrying out such activities will in principle have to obtain authorisation from a chemical inspector. Such a trader was also obliged to provide the inspector with information by 31 March of each year for the previous year on his activity. It is also to keep records of new psychoactive substances according to the date of acquisition or entry into possession for 5 years from the end of the calendar year. An entrepreneur receiving a new psychoactive substance is to draw up a declaration for each transaction of the use of a new psychoactive substance. The draft law also regulates the obligation to report cases to the National Sanitary Inspection Units to be poisoned by a substitute or a new psychoactive substance. A medicinal product treating a suspected substitute or new psychoactive substance will be required to report this to the competent national health inspector immediately, but not later than 48 hours after suspicion of poisoning or death due to poisoning. The county health inspector shall immediately make this notification available to the competent regional health inspector and the chief health inspector who will verify the data made available. The Chief Sanitary Inspector will be required to draw up a report by 31 March each year on the poisoning of a substitute or a new psychoactive substance. Under the new regulations, boosters will be treated by law on an equal footing with drugs, e.g. The import, export, transport, intra-Community acquisition or intra-Community supply of new psychoactive substances will be subject to fines, penalties for restrictions on liberty or imprisonment up to the age of 5 years, and thus the same as in the case of narcotic drugs or psychotropic substances. Criminal liability will also be exercised by drug dealers, who will be facing up to 12 years in prison for the distribution of boosters. In addition, criminal regulations will apply to holders of prohibited substances which will be punishable by fines, imprisonment or imprisonment up to the age of 3. Where a minor amount of a new psychoactive substance intended for personal use would be the subject of an act, it will be possible to terminate the proceedings in the light of certain circumstances. In addition, the new legislation is intended to provide legal assistance to drug addicts. The addiction to new psychoactive substances will be treated, rehabilitated, as will drug addiction. In view of the positive effects of the proposed amendments, I propose that the High Sejm adopt the attached draft law. Thank you very much.

The contested law introduces a cross-linking and complementing of the preventive effects adopted in municipal anti-drug programmes and anti-drug measures in schools and educational institutions. Its important dimension is also to clarify the rules governing the functioning of the State Sanitary Inspection or the regulations relating to the collection of data in the field of clinical toxicology. A number of changes to the revised process have been reflected in the work of the Parliamentary Group for the past 2 years. Troubleshooting the problems of addiction, which I chair. On this basis, too, it can be assumed that the proposed guidelines for regulatory changes meet the challenges of this issue. Nevertheless, some aspects of the bill should still be examined. In the case of a Charter for the assessment of a new psychoactive substance, consideration may be given to extending the list of recommendations to include social risks associated with the use of the substance, as well as to practical conclusions, if any, for example. to social education, prevention, harm reduction, prevention or therapy. The cooperation of the moderators of municipal anti-drug programmes with schools is also an appropriate solution, but knowledge of the implementation of anti-drug programmes at the level of municipal authorities suggests others which would be more precise in terms of substantive recommendations. You can e.g. consider the introduction of an additional component of assistance or control in the development of strategies and the selection of preventive programmes. At this point, it is worth noting that the transfer of knowledge about the harmfulness of drugs itself is ineffective in the prevention of the fact that gaining information is not tantamount to changing behaviour. However, it is the most widely used prevention strategy, also in schools, because it seems to be the simplest and thus the most accessible. For example, the challenge is greater. change of normative beliefs or shape the health-promoting attitudes of the audience. Modern prevention programmes, which have the ambition to change the behaviour of consumers, must take into account the active role and impact on and through parents.
