ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u713	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a draft law amending the law on combatants and certain victims of war repression and post-war periods and some other laws contained in print No. 2671. This is an important project, expected by the combatants, the disabled, the soldiers, the miners, the blind victims of war activities. You have a detailed justification in the draft law, so I will only focus on presenting the most important changes that are contained in the draft law. The bill deals with the area of rights of combatants and persons who were subjected to military repression and postwar periods, soldiers of military substitute service forced to work in coal mines, quarryings, uranium ore plants and construction battalions, persons deported and deported to forced labour by the Third Reich, the USSR, civilian blind victims of war activities, as well as anti-communist opposition activists and those repressed for political reasons. With regard to regulations on combatants and repressed persons, the proposed solutions aim to update and clarify the rules, to remove non-application provisions, to simplify procedures for the recognition of the application for the status of combatant and victims of repression, to allow the renewal of administrative proceedings in the event of new documents on a longer period than provided for in the Code of Administrative Procedure. As regards war disability, the proposed arrangements relate to the grant, in addition to their invalidity pension, of an allowance of PLN 850 to that pension, which is valued on the basis and within the time limit for pensions. This solution is the implementation of the declaration of the Council of Ministers expressed in its position towards the Commission's draft laws and the State's commitment to the special care of this social group. It is estimated that this entitlement will be covered by around 2.5 thousand. Invalids of war. In the case of military substitute soldiers employed in coal mines, quarrying, uranium ore plants and construction battalions, the proposed amendment aims to eliminate the variation in the amount of the money provision currently dependent on the number of months of operation, but no more than in total in 20 months. As a result of this change all eligible soldiers will receive a maximum amount of PLN 215.84. In addition to this benefit and already available a lump sum of PLN 168,71 the eligible persons are to be entitled to a compensatory allowance of PLN 32,38. Forced soldiers are also to be given priority rights in environmental social protection and special rights in the field of health services. The changes introduced will align the powers of forced soldiers with those of the oppressed. The number of beneficiaries of these solutions is estimated at around 17 000. In turn, the civilian blind victims of war activities are to be extended the scope of their rights by the right to a lump sum of PLN 168,71. In this respect, this social group will have powers similar to those of combatants or forced personnel. This entitlement is to be covered by approximately 230 people. The proposed changes also concern granting soldiers forced employment, civilian blind victims of war activities and persons deported and deported to forced labour by the Third Reich and the USSR the right to one-off or periodic monetary assistance granted by the Head of the Office for Combatants and Repressed Persons. On the other hand, as regards anti-communist opposition activists and those repressed for political reasons, the proposed changes regulate the convergence of the powers to provide money with the powers of the combatants and those repressed. The project also includes arrangements to allow pension authorities to provide cash benefits to those persons, whether or not resident abroad, excluding combatants and those already repressed enjoying such a right. Those who have been disabled as a result of the activity of the combatant and who have been issued the card of the oppressed person will automatically be able to receive a book of war invalids without being required to appear before the medical committees. The condition for such exchange of documents will be the submission to the pension authority of a certificate from the Head of the Office for Combatants and Repressed Persons that the stay in captivity, prison or camp was a consequence of the applicant's carrying out a combatant activity or activity equivalent to it within the meaning of the Act on Combatants and certain persons who were victims of war and post-war repression. The proposed amendments also contain regulations adapting to the group of provisions on personal income tax, family benefits, public-funded health care benefits, pensions and social insurance fund. We assume that the Act will enter into force on 1 January 2019. The planned funds for 2019 are almost PLN 40 million. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I ask the High Sejm to adopt a government proposal for a law on combatants and certain victims of war and post-war repression and some other laws. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u714	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Ms Anita Czerwińska, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u761	Mr. Marshal! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on print No. 2589, i.e. the Act on ratification of the Agreement between the Republic of Poland and Mongolia on social security, signed in Warsaw on 24 January 2018. The Act gives its consent to the ratification by the President of the Republic of Poland of a bilateral agreement between the Republic and Mongolia. So far, there have been no legal regulations in the field of social security between the Republic of Poland and Mongolia, which led to the fact that both Polish citizens in Mongolia, as well as the Mongols in Poland, had no right, for example. to sickness or retirement benefits despite the payment of contributions in the course of the work carried out. The right to social security is a fundamental human right, so it is good that such an agreement has been established. It is only a pity that the ratification of the contract signed, I will remind you, in January 2018 she had to wait until 6 months in government drawers. It is even more surprising that the law allowing these provisions to be introduced contains only 2 articles, the first of which is the agreement for ratification and the second is the standard deadline for implementation - nothing complicated. In social and civic matters, this government has shown exceptional tardiness. If it were, for example. For the courts, the bill would have been in force by now. Bilateral agreements are standard international agreements, the main objective of which is to coordinate social security systems that provide effective insurance coverage for citizens of both countries. The ratification of the agreement will provide social security for the citizens of both countries to the extent specified in the agreement, and the ratification procedure proposed by the Council of Ministers does not raise objections according to the BAS opinion. Today it will be a real support for about 350 Mongolian citizens living and working in Poland, but also for the remaining 250 students from Mongolia. Unfortunately, the Ministry has no information on the number of Poles staying in Mongolia who will benefit from the change introduced today. The total cost charged to the budget due to the introduction of this agreement was estimated by the Ministry at PLN 3.5 million per year. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will vote in favour of this law and agree to ratify the agreement.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u762	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Currently, Polish citizens who work or live in Mongolia do not have such an insurance contract. This project is intended to bind our country and Mongolia with an agreement that will enable Poles living and working in Mongolia to be covered by an insurance agreement. Moreover, the signing of such an agreement between our countries will have a significant impact on the image of our country among entrepreneurs who want to operate in Mongolia. This will open the way for many entrepreneurs who are not convinced to invest in Mongolia, who have not yet wanted to risk investing in Mongolia because of the unclear social security situation. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u763	High Seym! On behalf of the club PSL - ED, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law on ratification of the Agreement between Rzeczpospolita Polska and Mongolia on social security, signed in Warsaw on 24 January 2018. The economic exchange between Poland and Mongolia is developing systematically. Between 2000 and 2014, the number of Polish exporters in the Mongolian market increased from 72 to 252. In Mongolia there are now about 30 companies with Polish capital. The prospects for the expansion of trade increased further after the signing of 9 May 2017. The Agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Government of Mongolia on the granting of loans under tied aid. Data from the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy show that currently 337 Mongolian citizens in Poland are covered by Polish pension insurance. With the development of economic cooperation between our countries, this figure may increase, especially due to the influx of Mongolian citizens to work in the wider healthcare sector. In view of these circumstances, the Mongolian authorities have taken the initiative to conclude a bilateral social security agreement. After several years of negotiations, such an agreement was signed and submitted to the High Sejm for ratification. The PSL Club - ED will vote in favour of authorising the President of the Republic of Poland to ratify the above-mentioned agreement, assuming that the right to social security belongs to fundamental human rights. The practical consequence of the entry into force of the above-mentioned agreement will be the possibility of taking into account periods of insurance in the territory of the other Contracting Party in order to determine the right to social security benefits and their amount. At present, in determining the right to benefits and their amount, each country shall take into account only the periods spent in its territory. As a result, the persons concerned often cannot obtain the right to benefits, particularly long-term benefits such as pensions, either by applying only Polish - or Mongolian - insurance regulations, or by applying the principle of aggregation of insurance periods. Under this Agreement it will also be possible to transfer social security benefits to persons who have moved to the territory of the other State. Currently, people residing in Mongolia who have obtained the right to Polish pensions cannot receive them at their place of residence, but only through an authorised person or bank account located in Poland. This is undoubtedly a handicap that should be removed in the 21st century. An important argument for ratification of the above-mentioned agreement is the current obligation to pay double social security contributions to companies operating in both countries. This increases the costs of their operation and inhibits the movement of workers. In view of the above arguments, the PSL Club - ED will vote in favour of the bill submitted.

The existence of a bilateral agreement concluded between Poland and Estonia is contested by the European Commission because the agreement is incompatible with European Union law. Similarly, all other agreements to promote and protect each other's investments between the Member States of the European Union are contested. The Court of Justice of the European Union also expressed its opinion on this issue, which, in response to the question referred for a preliminary ruling, stated that the investment promotion and protection agreements were incompatible with Union law. This agreement was intended to provide both the Polish and Estonian parties with full protection and the possibility of redress against an international investment tribunal in the event of a violation of the rights of one of the parties. The protection of investment through international arbitration was intended to be a kind of fuse for both foreign investors in Poland and for Polish investors abroad. The European Commission has serious concerns that agreements to promote and protect each other's investments, including the abovementioned. the agreement and their individual provisions are in line with the provisions of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union. Modern will support this project. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u774	Thank you very much. There are no questions this time, which is why there is no answer. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u775	Mr. Marshal! Minister! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I would like to inform you that our circle supports further proceding on the proposed bill on ratification of the Agreement between the Republic of Poland and the Republic of Estonia on the amendment and termination of the Agreement between the Republic of Poland and the Republic of Estonia on mutual support and protection of investment, drawn up in Warsaw on 6 May 1993. Today's laws were constructed in a different political and economic reality. It was a time when we wanted to attract foreign investors and ensure their legal security. Today we are a law-abiding country, a member of the European Union, which duplicates the provisions of the current laws. We don't need legal duality. We have signed treaties that ensure equal treatment by courts for all investors in the rule of law state of Poland. The bill has no economic or social consequences. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u776	Statement by Mr Jacek Protasiewicz, parliamentary club PSL - ED, item 27 of the agenda discussed on 19 July 2018. High Seym! On behalf of the club PSL - ED, I have the honour to present my position towards the Government's draft law on ratification of the Agreement between the Republic of Poland and the Republic of Estonia on the amendment and termination of the Agreement between the Republic of Poland and the Republic of Estonia on mutual support and protection of investment, drawn up in Warsaw on 6 May 1993. The proposed law contains the consent of the President of the Republic of Poland to ratify the Agreement referred to in the title of the Act. This will be the final part of the procedure initiated by the note of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs of the Republic of Poland of 20 October 2017, addressed to the Embassy of the Republic of Estonia, in which the Polish Ministry of Foreign Affairs proposed the conclusion of the Agreement referred to above. 19 March 2018 The Ministry of Foreign Affairs of the Republic of Estonia sent in response to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs of the Republic of Estonia a note in which the Republic of Estonia agreed to conclude such an Agreement under conditions proposed by the Republic of Poland. The main condition formulated by the Polish party was the expectation that no provision of the Agreement of 6 May 1993 would remain in force. It is necessary to explain the circumstances which led to the issuing of a note of the Polish Ministry of Foreign Affairs. The agreement, which was proposed by the Polish party, was concluded before Poland and Estonia entered the EU. Its aim was to encourage Polish investors to invest in Estonia and Estonians in Poland and to guarantee them legal security through the possibility of resolving disputes before the international arbitration tribunal. Following the entry of Poland and Estonia into the EU and the adoption by both countries of the acquis communautaire, the need for such a legal guarantee of the security of investment was no longer justified. Moreover, according to the European Commission, as confirmed by one of the judgments of the Court of Justice of the EU, these agreements may be incompatible with EU law and the Commission has therefore called on all the governments of the EU Member States which have concluded such agreements among themselves to resolve them. One of the main arguments of the European Commission is that the agreements in question - because of excessive protectionism and the possibility of redress against an international investment tribunal - may infringe the Treaty rules on the movement of capital, freedom of establishment and freedom to provide services. The European Commission also considers that these agreements, called intra-EU BITs, are also contrary to the fundamental principles of EU law, such as the principle of primacy, unity, efficiency and certainty of EU law, and the settlement of disputes through international investment arbitrage appears to be contrary to the exclusive competence of EU courts. It is difficult to disagree with this argument from the European Commission. It is also shared by the Polish government, which further argues that the termination of these agreements should not have negative political and social consequences. On the contrary, as the Polish Government claims, it is to be expected that the Agreement will be welcomed by public criticism in Poland of intra-EU BIT agreements and international investment arbitration. In the written justification attached to the bill, the Government also informs the High Sejm that it has conducted consultations with national economic authorities. Despite some reservations on their part regarding the elimination of the possibility of resolving disputes through international investment arbitration, the government ensures that neither Polish nor Estonian investors were directed on the basis of the above mentioned. Contracts of claims to international arbitration tribunals against the Republic of Estonia and the Republic of Poland respectively. Taking all these arguments into account, the PSL Club - ED will vote in favour of the draft law which is the subject of this debate.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u812	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I submit a report to the Committee on Culture and Media about the draft resolution on the establishment of 2019 as the Year of the Lublin Union. The Commission carried out its first reading, examined the draft submitted on 9 May this year and adopted the resolution as follows: In 1569 an act of real Polish-Lithuanian union was signed in the presence of the king of Poland, the great Lithuanian prince Zygmunt Augustus at the Grand Sejm in Lublin. Two countries: The Crown of the Kingdom of Poland and the Grand Duchy of Lithuania have established this act 'one inseparable and various body' - the Republic of both Nations. It has survived for over 200 years, and it has only been vanquished by the combined forces of three invaders. Professor Władysław Konopczyński wrote: 'Never in history have separate nations entered into a more lasting wedding with less pressure. It happened as desired by hundreds of thousands, with only a handful of privileged ones and their pride. It must have been felt, and in the isolated Polock and Smolensk, and in the capitals of all countries, that there was not a slight administrative change here... but an act of great historical significance. After a year, Europe could distinguish between the Polish method of union and the Moscow Ivan, who had driven every train to Poland out of its heads with thousands of unparalleled executions." The Polish-Lithuanian Union in Lublin was one of the most important events of the century, not only for connected countries, but for the whole of Central and Eastern Europe. Its effects went far beyond political or economic importance. It is an act which has written in gold letters in the history and culture of the peoples of the Republic of Poland. 'The Lublin Union is one of the brightest rays in Polish history, because it stated again that not by invasions, not by partitions, but by mutual friendship the Polish state was expanding and powerful, combining equals with free and free' - reminded Prof. Feliks Koneczny. The conclusion of the Lublin Union is taking on a different meaning today. St. John Paul II. In 2003 during a meeting with fellow countrymen gathered in St. On the occasion of the 25th anniversary of the pontificate, Peter emphasized that Poland was always an important part of Europe and 'today it cannot be excluded from this community, which, although on various levels, is experiencing crises, but which is one family of nations, based on a common Christian tradition'. In the famous sentence: 'From the Lublin Union to the European Union', he committed us to actively building a community of spirit based on values that allowed us to survive decades of programming atheisation. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, convinced of the special importance of the act of 1569 for our Homeland and for Europe, establishes the year 2019 as the Year of the Lublin Union. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Committee on Culture and Media requests the High Sejm to adopt the draft resolution submitted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u816	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I am pleased to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club towards the parliamentary draft resolution on the establishment of the year 2019 as the Year of the Lublin Union. On 1 July 1569 an act of Polish-Lithuanian Union was signed at the Sejm in Lublin. Until 1795, the Lublin Union was one of the pillars of the Republic of Poland of both Nations. Polish-Lithuanian relations have always been complicated, and the year 1569 was very turbulent. The session of the Sejm in Lublin lasted for more than six months. After a long effort, Lithuanian gentlemen made an act of swearing in a union called the Lublin Union on 1 July 1569. The principles of Polish-Lithuanian coexistence were laid down there, the political separation of Lithuania was abolished, both legal systems were combined. There was a common Sejm and Senate, a common military policy, foreign policy, currency and crest, while separate were offices, army, judiciary and treasure. The conclusion of the Lublin Union was one of the most important events of the century, not only for connected countries, but for the whole of Central and Eastern Europe. The Union was of great political, economic and cultural importance. The Lithuanian nobles took over Polish and Western patterns, and the Polish side took over Eastern and Oriental patterns. The United State has become a bridge between the East and the West, an area of cultural exchange of unprecedented intensity. There was a polonization of Lithuanian elites, which gave up their own traditions, their own language and culture. Polish language, Latin, clothes and style of praising were taken over. At that time, the Lublin Union was a model of political and legal solutions. In 2019, 450 years have passed since the conclusion of this union. In view of the above, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will be in favour of adopting a resolution on the establishment of the year 2019 as the Year of the Lublin Union. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This enabled the creation and continuation of a multicultural and multireligious organism, which in times of persecution presented a different attitude. An important result of the Lublin Union was the dissemination of knowledge about the Republic of Poland and the cultural impact on the further territories of Europe. Lublin was awarded the European Heritage Mark to commemorate this event, and several hundred years later similar assumptions were at the heart of the European Community's concept. Today, in Central and Eastern Europe, the Lublin Union is also an idea of economic and political cooperation that opposes Eastern narratives and principles. The Polish-Lithuanian Union became a power at the time, which threatened Moscow in particular. Its durability was exceptional, only the partitions in the second half of the 18th century, preceded by numerous wars with the Ottoman Empire and Russia, destroyed it. Nevertheless, the idea connecting Poland and Lithuania, unique in its essence, survived as a symbol of a consciously formed political community by two nations. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u822	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present the report of the Committee on Culture and Media about the draft resolution on the establishment of 2019 by Gustav Herling-Grudziński. The Committee on Culture and Media, after first reading and considering this draft resolution at its meeting on 9 May 2018, established the following text of the resolution with a proposal to be adopted by the High House. The resolution of the Polish Sejm on the establishment of the year 2019 by the Year of Gustav Herlinga-Grudziński. 20 May 2019 will be 100 years since the birth of Gustav Herling-Grudziński - one of the most outstanding Polish writers of the 20th century. The fates and works of Gustav Herling-Grudziński bear witness to the sufferings and deeds of a man who experienced times of totalitarian violence and a crisis of values. Prisoner of the Soviet Lag, soldier of the Anders Army, participant of the Battle of Monte Cassino, emigrant, co-worker of Culture and Radio Free Europe, involved in helping the Polish anti-communist opposition, but most of all valued in the Homeland and the world author of the book 'An Other World. The Soviet records, moving stories and the 'Write of the Night' - Gustaw Herling-Grudziński with extraordinary courage describes the tragedy of a man seeking moral order and truth. The book 'Another World' was the first in world literature, artistically perfect, full of mercy and hope to bear witness to the Martyrology of Soviet prisoners. It was written by only a 30-year-old author who himself experienced the cruelty of the communist place of execution. The more bitter the writer was when some of Europe's intellectual elites refused to acknowledge his relationship. The stories of Gustav Herling-Grudziński are a deeply rooted collection of tales about metaphysical secrets of man's fate in European culture. The author is fascinated by the struggle with the mystery he described in Shakespeare's words: 'We are of the matter from which dreams are made'. His' Written Log at night' is an extremely original, estheistic, personal chronicle of the history of Poland and Europe in the 20th century, seen from emigration by the eyes of the Polish patriot and European. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, in recognition of the word and act of the great writer, establishes the year 2019 as the Year of Gustav Herling-Grudziński. The Committee on Culture and Media requests that this draft be adopted as just presented. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The appeal was not a simple call to women, and Herling was well aware of it. His story of complications was consistent with the complexity of the situation. The writer put the Church to the test, faking a situation in which one of the victims of rape dies after birth, which triggers a beatification and sanctifying process. Beatification does not occur because a woman just before she died - as her face points out, crooked by some disgusting grimace - probably blasphemed. In this way the writer presented a simple and very high account to the Church - he held responsible for the moral call formulated by the Pope, and at the same time pointed out that the Church still sees in the victims of rape a trace of moral abomination. If it wasn't, what would the face matter? If suffering is sufficient to recognize holiness, all raped women should be raised to the altars. If that's not the case, what are the appeals for? I will leave you with this statement, although I do not believe that it will lead you to a deeper reflection. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u835	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of Kukiz'15 a position regarding the establishment of 2019 in the Year of Gustav Herling-Grudziński. Gustav Herling-Grudziński was born in 1919 in Kielce. Immediately after the outbreak of World War II, he founded the Polish People's Independence Action. He was trying to get to the West. During his attempt to enter Lithuania in March 1940, he was captured by the NKVD and sentenced to 5 years in the Soviet Lags. After the protest hunger in January 1942, he was released from the camp and joined the army of General Władysław Anders, with whom he left the Soviet Union. Herling-Grudziński fought in the Second Corps, among others. At Monte Cassino, for which he received the cross of Virtuti Militari. In 1947 he and Jerzy Giedroyzyk founded the Institute of Literature in Rome and published a monthly magazine "Culture. " Between 1952 and 1955, he worked at the Polish Radio Free Europe radio station in Munich. Since 1955, he has moved to Naples permanently. Herling-Grudziński is one of the most important authors of Polish contemporary literature. He was a writer, an essayist, a critic and a journalist. His work was honoured with numerous awards, Grudziński received among others. PEN Club award and honorary doctorate at the Jagiellonian University. For political reasons, the writer's works were not printed in Poland until 1988. The most important work in Grudziński's work, i.e. Another world, describes the author's experience in the Soviet Lagoon. Club Kukiz'15 supports the draft resolution submitted.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u837	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the report of the Committee on Culture and Media about the draft resolution on the establishment of 2019 Year of the Silesian Uprising. The Marshal of the Sejm on 8 May sent a draft resolution on the establishment of 2019 as the Year of the Silesian Uprising, print No. 2515 to the Committee on Culture and Media of the Sejm on 27 April. The Committee on Culture and Media discussed the draft at its meeting on 9 May and, after one amendment had been tabled unanimously, i.e. without votes against, recommends the High House to adopt a resolution, print No 2532, as follows: Silesia has just given the whole of Poland an example of perseverance and steadfastness in the service of national-state ideas' - Eugeniusz Kwiatkowski. The Silesian Uprisings are extremely important events of the history of Silesia and the history of Poland. The three Silesian Uprisings of 1919, 1920, 1921 carry a message for future generations. The uprising struggle for the return to Poland of Piastów Górny Śląska was characterized by exceptional consistency and effectiveness. Despite the several centuries of separation of the region from the rest of the homeland and application by the German administration of Germanization policy, Poland survived. It was mainly thanks to women - Silesian mothers, who from generation to generation passed on knowledge of Polish language to their children and grandchildren, mainly through prayer, traditions and customs, and also taught their children Polish history. It was them - Silesian mothers who raised Polish patriots who did not hesitate to grab their weapons in the hour of trial and fight for Silesia to return to Poland. Thanks to the heroic raids of Silesian insurgents headed by Wojciech Korfanty and Alfons Zkopniok, their determination and generous blood donation, a significant part of Upper Silesia was eventually incorporated into the reborn Second Republic in 1922. A prominent statesman and great leader of the Silesian people Wojciech Korfanta lived his life sacrificially in continued service to Silesia and Poland. During the Silesian Uprising he proved to be a brilliant strategist and diplomat. He organized the Polish administration in insurgent areas, did not allow the destruction of the Upper Silesian economic potential and led to the transfer of it to Poland in full production efficiency, which in a fundamental way contributed to the economic development of the Second Republic. It was the potential of Upper Silesia that enabled, among others. the construction of the coal bus and facilitated the construction of Gdynia or the Central Industrial District. The Silesian Uprisings are one of the most beautiful cards in the history of the Polish nation, they are the final chord of the process of unification of Polish lands in the post-partition period. The patriotism of the insurgents, their love of polishness, reason and fortitude create a constant pattern to follow. Let the bravery, faithful service and love for the Homeland shown by the Silesian insurgents be a sign of conduct and a model of life for future generations, and the insurgent acts should remain an expression of the highest patriotism in the national and state-created dimension. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland expresses its gratitude for the contribution of generations of the Silesian people to the fight for the independence of the Republic and then its reconstruction, and for the significant influence on the shape of the borders of the reborn and independent Polish state and establishes 2019 Year of the Silesian Uprising. The Commission requests the adoption of a draft resolution on the content presented. Thank you. [[Applause]]

According to the provisions of the peace agreement ending the First World War signed on 28 June 1919, the state of Upper Silesia, which was then a multinationality area, was to decide the plebiscite. This uprising proved to be victorious for Poland. Its direct effect was better for our homeland to divide the Upper Silesian land despite the defeated plebiscite on 20 March 1921. The role of Polish national activists with Wojciech Korfanty in these important events for the Republic of Poland remains undisputed. Today, at the threshold of the hundredth anniversary: three Silesian Uprisings, giving the Law Sejm a non-existent yet then Silesian Voivodeship organic statute, which introduced the broad autonomy of Upper Silesia, plebiscite, and finally connecting the eastern part of Silesia with the young Polish state on 20 June 1922, we owe honor and glory to those who made it possible for the Republic to become a strong and modern state. Worshiping the memory of the Polish Silesian uprisings and their heroes, we must not forget, however, that for many Upper Silesian families it was also a time of difficult decisions and personal dramas, because on the other side also the Silesians fought, often members of the same families, or remained, living in harmony so far, neighbors. Also to them and their descendants, regardless of the then national and state elections, there is today due respect, in the spirit of reconciliation of all citizens of the present Republic of Poland. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u848	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the report of the Committee on Culture and Media about the draft resolution on the establishment of 2019 in the Year of Anna Valentynowicz, print No. 2517. The Marshal of the Sejm sent the above draft resolution to the Committee on Culture and Media for first reading on 8 May 2018. It is a parliamentary draft resolution prepared by the club Law and Justice. The Committee on Culture and Media, after first reading and considering this draft resolution at its meeting on 9 May 2018, also following a few minor corrections, unanimously adopted the above draft resolution and asked the High Sejm to approve the attached draft. The draft resolution recommended by the Committee on Culture and Media reads as follows: The resolution of the Polish Sejm on the establishment of the year 2019 by Anna Valentynowicz. 15 August 2019 marks the 90th anniversary of the birth of Anna Valentynowicz, an independent activist of Free Trade Unions and a co-founder of the Independent Self-Governmental Trade Union (Solidarity). 'The history of Anna Valentynowicz's life - closely linked to the fate of Poles in the 20th century - is like a collective portrait of Polish workers getting up from their knees to rebel against communism. Always modest, she treated her life as a service," recalls historian Prof. Sławomir Cenckiewicz, calling Anna Valentynowicz Anna Solidarity. In 1970 she participated in anti-communist labour protests in Tri-City and in a strike in Gdańsk Shipyard. Since 1978, acting in the Free Trade Unions of the Coast, she has made the apartment available as a contact point, edited and sold the independent magazine "The Coast Workers" and co-organized the celebration of December'70 and cultivated the memory of the victims of the communist murder on workers. She's been stalked and detained for 48 hours many times. In August 1980 Anna Valentynowicz was fired. This was the direct cause of the outbreak of the 14th August strike in Gdańsk Shipyard. The first demand of the protesters was to bring her back to work. Anna Valentynowicz, together with Alina Pieńkowska, led to the continuation of the strike on 16 August and turned it into a solidarity strike with workers who strike at other workplaces. The growing wave of strikes and protests in many places in Poland forced the Communists to negotiate and conclude an agreement with the strikers and led to the establishment of the NSZZ 'Solidarity'. After the martial law in December 1981, she was interned and imprisoned. In the following years she continued her activity for Poland's independence. She opposed the round table and consistently demanded a return to the ideas and demands of 'Solidarity'. In free Poland for many years it remained underestimated and only in 2006 the President of the Republic of Poland, Lech Kaczyński, awarded it with the Order of the White Eagle. She died in Smolensk on April 10, 2010. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, in recognition of the merits of Anna Valentynowicz for the creation of a great movement of 'Solidarity' and regaining independence by Poland and paying tribute to her steadfast attitude, establishes the year 2019 with Anna Valentynowicz. On behalf of the Parliamentary Committee on Culture and Media, I have the honour to recommend to the High House the adoption of a parliamentary draft resolution on the establishment of the year 2019 by Anna Valentynowicz, Anna Solidarity. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u865	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the parliamentary draft resolution on the establishment of 2019 in the Year of Anna Valentynowicz. It was from Anna Valentynowicz that August 1980 began, it was from her that solidarity began. It was from her that the process of dropping communist eyeballs from Poland and cut through the iron curtain of Europe began. It was she who, in a symbolic and real way, initiated the work of crushing the Soviet empire, which surprised the whole world. It was astonished by both form and scale. This was not the rage of the crowds and the canon of gunshots, but the power of the spirit, the civil courage of often simple, but great people. The most significant figure of this in the history of the world of unprecedented movement was Anna Valentynowicz - a figure then shown in the media of all continents: a quiet, silent worker, often holding a cross or rosary in her hands. Because those were the keys to freedom in Poland. Of Anna Valentynowicz it is often and how well it is said: the mother of Solidarity. Or Anna Solidarity. In fact, that's how her name could sound. Valentinovich - it meant exactly as much as Solvency. She never pushed herself on a candlestick. She didn't aspire to the role of leader. She was a spiritual and real leader. Although, above all, she was a good man, a man of heart, a man of fortress work, devoted to the welfare of every needy helper and at the same time to the consecrated homeland. She was one of the most important figures of the Free Trade Unions of the Coast. Countless times repressed, imprisoned, harassed. In the most difficult times, in months of hopelessness, she has always lifted everyone up, never lost faith. I'm afraid to think about what our story would have been like if it hadn't been for her. She was a great inspiration to everyone who met her. She was a source of strength, even though she herself was a sick and suffering person in the last few years of her life. There was nothing to her that meant immeasurably tossed in her direction of insults and lies, including the far-reaching pursuits to remove her role, to erase her historical merits, to replace her with some other false legend. She didn't even react to all those vile steps to rob her of her life's work. Because not for fame, not for glory, not for any, even the smallest of her own benefits, she risked herself to death, and she rubbed herself to death. The awareness that Anna Valentynowicz is not only the greatest of heroes August 1980 is far too small in Poland. Anna Solidarity is also one of the largest forms of.Solidarity. She became one immediately when she was in a state of war to leave prison. Then again, as in August'80, she was not a person pushing into the role of a well-known leader. On the other hand, researchers of history who seek to recreate the history of trade unions and independent organizations fighting in Poland in the 1980s are constantly encountering traces of the countless activities of Anna Valentynowicz at the time. She was the inspiration for a lot of underground initiatives. It led to the meeting of those people whose combined forces then multiplied the potential of the solidarity underground. Always thinking about the safety and welfare of everyone except yourself. Never mind the memory of your role. And always devoted with her whole life to Poland and Poles. It's about Anna Valentinovich that almost everyone knows. Few people know, however, that Anna also cooperated with Solidarity Fighting, which was not only popular at the time, but was often also badly seen, also in opposition environments. I had the privilege of working with Anna in a conspiracy in the late '80s. She'd deliver safe premises, recommend people, get backup. I remember organizing one of the meetings of Ani Valentynowicz with the fighters Jadwiga Chmielowska and my current husband Roman, wanted by the Security Service at the time. Also the last conspiracy meeting with Kornel Morawiecki immediately prior to his taking up public activity after his illegal return to Poland. Anne is one of the few people awarded for these actions, for fighting for freedom and solidarity between people and nations - the honorary Cross of Solidarity Fighting. About Anna Valentynowicz you can talk for a long time, but without a shadow of doubt you can say that it is one of the most well-deserved, most noble figures of the history of Poland in the second half of the 20th century. She wasn't a politician. She wasn't a title activist. Yet, she played a colossal role and played only the best part. Her life is proof that you can be the man of the greatest format and the greatest merit, without having any institutionalized, formal high function. It would be an unforgivable sin not to cultivate the memory of Anna Valentynowicz properly. It would be a sin not so much against Anna Valentinovich himself. It would be a sin towards Poland and it would be an injury, although for many it would be unconscious, but it would be done to Poles themselves. Because not carefully enough remembering the great figure of our homeland, each Pole would lose himself with such neglect. I am convinced that this important gesture of the Polish Sejm will constitute the proper honoring of Anna Valentynowicz in the year of her 90th birthday. The Free and Solidarni Circle supports this draft resolution.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u866	We will proceed to the vote on this motion for a resolution in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 33 of the agenda: Report of the Committee on Culture and Communications on Parliamentary and Commission draft resolutions on the establishment of 2019 Year of Stanisław Moniuszko (prints No. 2166, 2167 and 2538). ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u867	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Media, it is my pleasure to present a report on draft resolutions on the establishment of 2019 as the Year of Stanisław Moniuszko. The Committee on Culture carried out its first reading on 10 May, prepared a resolution which is, of course, contained in the print presented to you. The immediate reason for this resolution is, of course, the anniversary which we will celebrate in 2019. This is the 200th anniversary of Stanisław Moniuszko's birthday, about which we would like to say in words written about Adam Mickiewicz: We all of him. This famous sentence today should be repeated about Stanisław Moniuszka - one of the greatest Polish composers and patriots. Because no one else, but he saved Polish folk culture from oblivion, immortalizing traditional songs in his songs. Each Pole grew up on the rocks or arias of Halki, each of us, without exception, knows at least from hearing Moniuszko's works, even if they do not immediately associate them with his name. The composer thus had an extraordinary influence on Polish cultural heritage, on national identity. Announcement of 2019 The Year of Stanisław Moniuszko is our duty, our historical duty. We must complete this in the year following the anniversary of the 100th anniversary of independence. Moniuszko next to Chopin is commonly called the greatest patriot composer. He did not choose emigration, but devoted himself to popularizing the national heritage on the spot, which was a difficult task at the time of the partitions. Chopin was a lyric, Moniuszko was an epic. He was the first to realise that combining music with poetry would be the most effective way to promote culture in society. By this method, not only folk melodies were disseminated, but also Polish literature. With full awareness he composed almost exclusively vocal works, in which words play a role no less than melody. In his letter to Jan Ignacy Kraszewski he wrote: German musicians choose poetry to inspire themselves, and their Schiller, Goethe and all the famous poets found the happiest tunes. It's sad to see that none of us who write music have ever tried their best in poetry from a man who looks like a singer. In his works he was stubbornly promoting the national theme, often consciously exposing himself to censored, assassins. He did not hide that his main goal was to encourage Poles to music together, especially singing, as the most effective way to maintain Polish cultural heritage. There is no exaggeration in saying that Moniuszko was the greatest cultural emissary of Polishness until independence was regained in 1918. This was particularly evident in the area of the Prussian partition. Here, as a German model, many amateur choirs and bands worked. Moniuszko's legacy was the basis of their repertoire, contributing to the building of Polish identity and pride in belonging to the nation. Moniuszko was the first composer to oppose the monopoly of Italian opera, which in the 19th century was the main element of cultural cosmopolitanism threatening Polish identity. He also tried, as far as possible, to promote his patriotic works in Europe. In Moscow, St. Petersburg and Prague, the famous Bedřich Smetana, a great Moniuszko fan, conducted the symphony orchestra. Stanisław Moniuszko was a versatile and hardworking artist. He left behind six operas, choral ballads, several operettas, many cantat-oratorial works, and had an invaluable impact on Polish pedagogy, which still remains current. His diary to learn harmony is still used in Polish music science. Moreover, it can impress contemporary Poles as a personality model of a teacher and creator. He began his career as an organist in Vilnius to become the main conductor of the Warsaw National Opera and lecturer of the Music Institute. He died prematurely, at the age of 53, fully creative. His funeral in Warsaw in June 1872 became a great patriotic manifestation, attended by several thousand people. So Moniuszka owes us not only compositions, but also a large part of our own identity. Unfortunately, the development of popular culture and mass media contributed to the disappearance of the popularity of classical music in general, including that based on family folk traditions. The announcement of 2019 by Stanisław Moniuszko should not be merely a symbol or an empty man, in order to best pay tribute to the composer by regularly reminding Poles of his work. Just as Moniuszko saved folk culture from oblivion 100 years ago, so today we must save his music from oblivion. The Committee on Culture and Media recommends that the High Sejm adopt this resolution after the discussion. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Stanisław Tyszka of the Sejm]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u871	In early May 2019 it will be 200 years since the birth of the outstanding Polish composer and patriot Stanisław Moniuszko. This great Polish composer was born in 1819 in the territory of today's Belarus, in a family strongly cultivating patriotism towards our country. Stanisław Moniuszko moved to history as the father of the Polish national opera. He created for heart-building in the times of partitions when Poland was not on the map of Europe and the world. His works are most prominent in the years preceding and immediately after the outbreak of the January Uprising, when, contrary to difficult social and political conditions, he created his most famous works - operas Halka and Scary Manor. Moniuszko's inspiration for music was folk culture. The composer's genius was the ability to combine folklore with elements of European opera: Italian, French and German. Choirs play an important role in Moniuszko's operas, e.g. Highlanders, as well as elements of Polish national dances such as polonaise, mazur, highlander dances. About how well he got his work into the taste of the audience not only Polish but also European, let it be borne out by the conquest of European capitals such as Prague and Moscow in the 1960s. 19th century He also left behind other capable composers. From 1864 he taught and lectured harmony, counterpoint and composition at the Music Institute in Warsaw. It was there that his student was another great composer, a great symphony artist Zygmunt Noskowski. He died of a heart attack in 1872 when he was only 52 years old. The Civic Platform Club of the Polish Republic expresses its support for the resolution of the parliament, which aims to commemorate the 200th anniversary of the birth of the outstanding Polish composer Stanisław Moniuszko. Thanks to the establishment of 2019 the Year of Stanisław Moniuszko will be as Poles an opportunity to get acquainted with the big achievements of our master. Through this resolution, we want the great Pole Stanisław Moniuszko to find in the memory of young Poles, but not only the right place, due to such a wonderful person.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u872	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on draft resolutions on the establishment of 2019 in the Year of Stanisław Moniuszko, prints No. 2166, 2167 and 2538. Stanisław Moniuszko was the creator of the Polish national opera and the main representative of vocal lyrics. The national character of his works was decided by the elements of Polish folklore and the discussed themes - folklore in Halce, noble traditions in the scary court, the beauty of Polish customs in the Countess. His opera features the richness of melodic invention, the relevance of the musical character characteristics and the colour of the genre scenes. This great Polish composer, conductor, educator and organist devoted his entire work to the idea of strengthening the hearts of enslaved compatriots. He could speak to his generation at the most tragic time for him. The years preceding the January Uprising and the years immediately after its fall were particularly difficult. It was at that time that the most famous works of his were created, the operas I have already mentioned: Halka and Scary Manor. Most of Moniuszko's compositions are songs, written to a large extent for home music and collected in subsequent volumes of the home singer, but the most important works in the composer's work are operas, reflecting such an important national trend in the music of the Romantic era in Slavic countries. He also wrote religious music, songs and songs for organs, wanting to raise the level of music performed in the church, and also, as he wrote, to deepen the spiritual experiences of the faithful. The instrumental music is much less important in his work - he has not written a single symphony, but his outstanding achievement is the fantastic overture of Fairytale. The creativity of Stanisław Moniuszko raised the artistic level of various musical genres and gained wide recognition in Polish society. It is commonly regarded as a pattern of Slavic music. Moniuszko was undoubtedly the most outstanding Polish composer in the second half of the 19th century, and after Fryderyk Chopin was the main representative of national style in Polish music. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support the proposed resolution.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-19-sejm-67-2.u874	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Kukiz'15 Club a position regarding the establishment of 2019 as the Year of Stanisław Moniuszko. Stanisław Moniuszko was one of the most outstanding composers of Polish romanticism. Already in his early youth he studied piano under the direction of August Freyer. After his return from his studies in Berlin in 1840. Moniuszko married Aleksandra Müller. The couple lived in Vilnius, where the composer was a piano teacher, as well as a church organist. Soon he began to give concerts in Vilnius and Russia, and after performing his opera, Halki was successful. He took the position of the first conductor of Polish operas at the Grand Theatre in Warsaw. The second spectacular success of the composer was the opera "The Terrible Court," which was created to sustain the spirit of Poles in difficult times after the January Uprising. Moniuszko was the greatest creator of the Polish national opera. Folk culture inspired his music. The composer was able to express human emotions, even character, personality of heroes. choirs play an important role in his operas, e.g. Highlanders, as well as elements of Polish national dances (poloneza, mazura, mountain dances). In addition to operas, the composer also created Polish songs, e.g. those issued in a major cycle entitled Home singer. The most famous are the melody for songs: "Shawk," "Know-I" this country, "O mother my lovely" or "Cossack." Moniuszko also created two cantatas of Crimean Sonnets and Spectre, as well as a ballad of Mrs. Twardowska. Club Kukiz'15 supports the draft resolution submitted.
